# audio/bt_sink_test -- hardware transcript, MIMXRT1170-EVKB + Murata 1XK (IW416) M.2 card, iPhone as the A2DP SOURCE
# Bench 2026-09-09.  Build: build-bench (real uartIW416_bt.bin, M2_BT_UART_DNLD, WAKE_PULSE, RTS_FLOW water 1, FAST_BAUD 3000000).
# Console: MCU-Link VCOM 115200 via tools/rt1170-console.py; heartbeat lines are SAMPLED below (every 30th block), events are complete.
# Three runs; the library moved between them (M2Radio 56bcc8d -> a70f927 -> 9f24315, evkb 3ad84b4 -> 205d36b):
#   run 1  M2_BT_FORGET_BONDS=ON, pin 56bcc8d: first pairing (Just Works), 13 min streaming, NO AVRCP (the phone never opened AVCTP, volume presses invisible),
#          ONE underrun + one overrun every ~10 s (the heartbeat print blocking loop() past the ring's cover); music clean by ear.
#   run 2  FORGET_BONDS=OFF, a70f927 (AVRCP TG record Category 2) + the bt_avrcp heartbeat line: stored-key reconnect (paired_by=stored) x3 boots,
#          the phone STILL never opened AVCTP; the 09:0x drop reason 0x16 was the user ending the connection to take a call.
#   run 3  FORGET_BONDS=ON then OFF, 9f24315 (sink opens AVCTP itself) + 4 KB console TX extension: pairing, volume buttons -> volume=N (96 lines,
#          audible), 13.5 min streaming incl. pause/resume (SUSPEND hold: ring frozen, no underruns counted), out-of-range -> reason 0x08 ->
#          listening; the phone does NOT re-page after a range loss (iOS: the user re-selects the speaker) -> tapped -> stored-key reconnect,
#          AVCTP re-opened, volume re-registered, streaming.  Music clean by ear throughout.
# MEASURED (run 3, 10-min window 12:38-12:48, music, one pause/resume): seqgaps 2 (both at edges), bad=0 l2drop=0 timeouts=0, links=1 lost=0,
#   under +103 / over +89 in PAIRED bursts of 11-17 every ~2 min plus singles (a 35-45 ms delivery gap then a catch-up burst overflowing the
#   16-slot ring -- the phone's delivery jitter, not clock drift), trim hunting 25-96 ppm around ~65 with instantaneous fill 11-15 (target 8).
#   The spec's under=0/over=0 target is NOT met; the audible acceptance is.  Start-up transient: ~27 underruns while the ring first fills.

# ===== RUN 4 (NEW-42 CONTROL ARM) -- bench 2026-09-09 evening =====
# Build: build-bench-control -- the NEW-41 configuration (RING 16 / TARGET 8 / no pre-fill) PLUS the NEW-42
#   instrument, from the same source as the change arm, one CMake flag apart:
#     -DBT_SINK_RING=16 -DBT_SINK_TARGET=8 -DBT_SINK_PREFILL=OFF
#     (+ real uartIW416_bt.bin, M2_BT_UART_DNLD/WAKE_PULSE/RTS_FLOW=ON RTS_WATER=1 FAST_BAUD=ON 3000000,
#      M2_BT_FORGET_BONDS=OFF).  btin = 0x2a00 in .bss; the change arm is 0x4a00, +8192 = 16 extra slots.
# WHY THIS ARM EXISTS: it measures the phone's ACTUAL inter-packet gap distribution -- the number that
#   SIZES TARGET, instead of the 35-45 ms estimate NEW-41 inferred from the burst shape -- and it is the
#   only thing that proves the new instrument fires in a regime already known to be bad.  A clean reading
#   on the change arm alone is equally consistent with a gap counter that never triggers.
#
# ** BENCH TRAP MET FIRST, AND IT IS NOT A NEW-42 DEFECT. **  bonds_boot=0 -- the board's bond store was
#   empty while the iPhone still held a stale link key for EVKB-SINK.  SSP then ran CORRECTLY all the way
#   to Just Works (io_cap_req -> user_conf_req numeric=19466 -> accept) and the PHONE failed it:
#   pairing_complete: status=0x05 (Authentication Failure).  BtLink's designed fallback then took the
#   legacy-PIN path -- which writes Write_Simple_Pairing_Mode=0 -- so SSP was OFF for the REST OF THE
#   SESSION and the next attempt went straight to pin_code_req with no IO-capability exchange at all,
#   which is the passcode prompt iOS showed.  ONE FAILED SSP POISONS EVERY LATER ATTEMPT UNTIL THE NEXT
#   BOOT, because only PREPARE re-enables it.  Fix at the bench: forget the device on the phone, then
#   press SW4 (PREPARE re-runs, ssp_mode mode=1) and pair fresh -- Just Works, status=0x00, first try.
#
# MEASURED (one continuous stream, 1585.5 s = 26.4 min -- the phone kept the A2DP stream open past the
#   music, so this is 2.6x the planned window and the counters are normalised per minute below):
#   pkts=68325 frames=546254 -> 7.995 frames/pkt (the EIGHT-blocks-per-packet fact, on this phone)
#   under=425 over=346 = 16.1 / 13.1 per MINUTE   (run 3 read 10.3 / 8.9 per min: same order, same
#     PAIRED shape -- the control arm reproduces the defect it exists to reproduce)
#   overev=101 -> 3.4 dropped frames per overrun event;  seqgaps=0 bad=0  (nothing lost on the air)
#   fillmin=1 fillmax=15 -- the 16-slot ring runs RAIL TO RAIL, and 15 is RING-1, the ceiling.  That is
#     spec s1's overrun mechanism caught in the act, not inferred.
#   trim -60..105 ppm;  reprimes=0 primed=0 prime_ms=0 (correct: the pre-fill is OFF in this arm)
#
# THE GAP DISTRIBUTION, which is what this arm was run for -- 68324 intervals, sum == pkts-1 EXACTLY:
#     <=30 ms   67307   98.512%
#     30-50 ms    988    1.446%
#     50-80 ms     27    0.040%
#     80-120 ms     2    0.003%
#     >120 ms       0    0%
#     worst gap    97 ms = 33.4 blocks
# TARGET DECISION: KEEP 16 (46.4 ms).  It rides out 99.958% of intervals (68295 of 68324).  The worst gap
#   is 97 ms -- BIGGER than NEW-41's 35-45 ms estimate -- but it happened TWICE in 26 minutes; covering it
#   would need TARGET 34 / RING 50, i.e. 99 ms of latency, to save two events.  The 29 intervals over
#   50 ms become BOUNDED re-primes (one counted underrun each) instead of underrun bursts, which is
#   exactly what the re-prime is for.  Spec s5 says to record such a tail, not to inflate TARGET until
#   the numbers look nice.
# PREDICTION FOR THE CHANGE ARM, written BEFORE it runs: over=0 (hard); reprimes ~29 per 26 min with
#   under ~= reprimes; fillmin >= 4 and fillmax <= 28; one prime_ms ~46 ms at START and no start-up burst.
#
# ----- RUN 4: events (complete) -----
RT1176 BT sink test up
serial2=up_115200
m2_wifi_reset=released
bt_wake=pulsed_10ms_low (GPIO_DISP_B2_13, mux returned to LPUART2_RTS_B)

bd_addr=A0:CD:F3:C5:B9:F3

# ===== RUN 5 (NEW-42 CHANGE ARM) -- bench 2026-09-10 =====
# Build: build-bench-change -- RING 32 / TARGET 16 / pre-fill ON, one CMake flag from RUN 4's control arm.
#   btin = 0x4a00 in .bss vs the control's 0x2a00 (+8192 = 16 extra slots).  Confirmed live at boot by the
#   instrument's own sentinel: bt_jit fillmin=32 pre-stream (the control arm read 16).
# Two boots, three streams, 50.1 min of audio total.  bonds_boot=1 -- the RUN 4 pairing survived the reflash
#   (the EEPROM store is outside the image's sectors), so every connect here was paired_by=stored.
#
# THE HEADLINE, against RUN 4's control arm normalised per minute of streaming:
#     control  26.4 min   under 425 (16.08/min)   over 346 (13.09/min)   overev 101   fillmax 15 (= RING-1)
#     change   50.1 min   under  10 ( 0.20/min)   over   3 ( 0.06/min)   overev   3
#     -> under 81x fewer, over 219x fewer.
#   boot 1  29.4 min  under 8  over 0  overev 0  reprimes 8  fill 1..27  gapmax 63 ms  prime_ms 87
#   boot 2  20.7 min  under 2  over 3  overev 3  reprimes 2  fill 1..31  gapmax 66 ms  prime_ms 89
#
# ** SPEC s5 IS NOT FULLY MET, and the failure is informative rather than marginal. **
#   over = 0                : boot 1 MET (zero in 29.4 min).  Boot 2 NOT MET -- 3 frames dropped.
#   under <= reprimes + 2   : MET both (8<=10, 2<=4).  The re-prime's 1:1 accounting holds exactly.
#   fillmax <= RING-4 = 28  : boot 1 MET (27).  Boot 2 NOT MET (31 = RING-1, the ceiling).
#   fillmin >= TARGET/4 = 4 : NOT MET (1, both) -- but THE CRITERION IS BROKEN, see below.
#   prime_ms ~ 46 ms        : NOT MET (87 / 89) -- benign, and the START-UP BURST IS GONE (0 here vs ~27
#                             in NEW-41 run 3; the first three heartbeats of boot 1 read under=0).
#   audible                 : clean throughout, EXCEPT the very first try (see below).
#
# ** ROOT CAUSE OF BOOT 2's OVERRUNS: A RE-PRIME FOLLOWED BY THE SOURCE'S CATCH-UP BURST. **  Traced in the
#   heartbeat series: fillmax sat at 25 with reprimes=1 for ~140 heartbeats, then in ONE window went
#   fillmax 25 -> 31, overev 0 -> 3, reprimes 1 -> 2, trimhi 152 -> 200.  The re-prime refills the ring to
#   TARGET (16) from fresh packets, and THEN the backlog the phone buffered during the gap lands on top of
#   it: 16 + burst > 31 and the surplus is dropped.  Boot 1 took 8 re-primes with no overrun and a peak of
#   27, so it turns on how much backlog the source has accumulated -- i.e. on gap length, not on luck.
#   NOTE this is NOT the rebuffer loop the design review modelled and excluded: there the burst arrived
#   DURING the re-prime and refilled it instantly (1 silent block).  Here the gap was long enough for the
#   re-prime to complete first.  The headroom above TARGET (RING-1-TARGET = 15 blocks) is what ran out.
#
# ** MY OWN fillmin CRITERION IS UNSOUND AND s5 SHOULD BE CORRECTED, NOT EXCUSED. **  fillMin samples the
#   PRE-POP fill on the pop branch only.  Approaching dry the sequence is ...fill=2 pop, fill=1 pop, then
#   the dry block -- so fillmin=1 is GUARANTEED on any run where the ring ever empties, and emptying is
#   exactly what triggers a re-prime.  `fillmin >= 4` is therefore equivalent to `reprimes == 0`; it never
#   measured habitual margin as s5 claims it does.  The instrument for that is the fill HISTOGRAM which
#   s6 lists as deferred -- deferred on the grounds that fillmin/fillmax answer the headroom question,
#   which this run refutes for the low side.
#
# ** AN AUDIBLE ARTEFACT THE COUNTERS DO NOT EXPLAIN. **  On the very first connection the listener heard
#   distortion resolving into silence, then both channels perfect, and it never recurred across three
#   streams and 50 minutes.  Boot 1's first stream reads under=0 for its first three heartbeats, over=0
#   for the whole boot, bad=0, and one seqgap somewhere in 76063 packets -- nothing there is a distortion
#   mechanism.  So it is NOT the ring, and it is NOT the decoder refusing frames.  Unattributed; it wants
#   its own look (WM8962 headphone-amp settling at first unmute is the obvious first suspect, and would be
#   independent of everything NEW-42 touches).  Recorded rather than explained away.
#
# ----- RUN 5: events (complete) -----

# ===== RUN 6 (NEW-42 dry-spell measurement) -- bench 2026-09-10 =====
# Same image as RUN 5 (RING 32 / TARGET 16 / pre-fill) PLUS the dry-spell instrument -- additive only, no
#   behavioural change, so the two runs are directly comparable.  26.3 min, one stream, paired_by=stored.
# PURPOSE: measure the quantity that sets the threshold N, which the re-prime itself TRUNCATES (spec s9.1):
#   the counter is consecutive EMPTY-ring update() calls IRRESPECTIVE of priming, excluding HELD, !m_live,
#   and everything before the START prime completes.
#
# MEASURED:  under=3  over=0  overev=0  reprimes=3  fill 1..27  gapmax 72 ms
#   dry spells:  drymax=9  drytot=11  d4=2  d8=0  d16=1  d32=0  dbig=0
#   -> THREE spells in 26.3 min: two of <=4 blocks, one of <=16; the LONGEST was 9 blocks (26 ms).
#
# ** THE INFERENCE WAS WRONG BY ~3x, WHICH IS WHY THIS RUN EXISTED. **  Spec s9.1 reasoned that the servo
#   holds fill at ~TARGET, so a gap of G blocks strands the ring for about G-16, putting the worst dry
#   spell near 25 blocks.  Measured: 9.  The error is visible in the same line -- fillmax=27, so the
#   operating point PLUS the 8-block sawtooth puts fill well above TARGET much of the time, and a 22-block
#   gap is simply absorbed.  The ring only empties when a gap lands on a trough, which is rare and shallow.
# ** AND over=0 HELD FOR 26.3 MINUTES WITH THREE RE-PRIMES. **  So RUN 5's boot-2 overrun was a re-prime
#   coinciding with a large backlog, not something every re-prime causes.  The threshold removes the
#   mechanism rather than a certainty.
# DECISION: N = 16 blocks (46.4 ms, and TARGET-sized).  It is clear above every spell this phone produced
#   (9) and still fires for a genuine stall.  Predicted effect: reprimes -> 0 here, over stays 0, and
#   `under` returns to counting BLOCKS for sub-threshold spells -- so it should read about drytot (11-ish
#   per 26 min), each an inaudible 2.9 ms tick, instead of 3 events.
#
# ** THE STARTUP ARTEFACT, HEARD AGAIN AND NOW PARTLY LOCALISED. **  The listener reports, for the second
#   run running and in the same shape: heavy distortion and choppiness at stream start, thinning to
#   complete silence, then crystal-clear from about 20 s -- identical to RUN 5, so NOT a regression from
#   the dry instrument.  What the capture rules OUT:
#     - it is NOT the ring starving.  under=1 for the whole first 24 s, and that one underrun is at t=1 s.
#       A 20-second choppy stretch would need hundreds.
#     - it is NOT the decoder or the air.  bad=0, seqgaps=0 in the window, and rms (measured at DECODE
#       time) reads 313 -> 1833 -> 2105 by t=3 s and is FLAT thereafter: the PCM entering the ring is
#       correct from ~2 s.
#   So the fault is DOWNSTREAM of the ring, in the clock/codec path.  The one thing changing over exactly
#   that window is the servo's PLL trim: -56 -> +148 ppm over the first nine seconds, written to the audio
#   PLL on every change (17 distinct values in the first 20 s against 8 in a later 20 s).  148 ppm is
#   0.26 cents and inaudible AS PITCH, so the mechanism would have to be the WM8962 losing lock on a
#   hiccuping SAI clock rather than the pitch shift itself.  HYPOTHESIS, not a diagnosis; the one-build
#   test is to disable the servo's PLL writes and listen.
#
# ----- RUN 6: events (complete) -----
